````math
$`C_{\rm visit}\coloneqq\frac{\log T+\log|{\mathcal{S}}|+\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{\gamma^{2}\min_{s\in{\mathcal{S}}}\sum_{h\in s}\mu_{c}(h)}`$
is the maximum gap between any two adjacent visits to an infoset.

#### Proof Sketch.

(i). Firstly, we show that the estimates in
[Algorithm 1](#alg1 "In 3 Q-Function based Regret Minimization (QFR) ‣ A Policy-Gradient Approach to Solving Imperfect-Information Games with Best-Iterate Convergence")
are unbiased so that the conditions of
[Lemma 4.3](#S4.Thmtheorem3 "Lemma 4.3 (Generalization of Proposition 1 in ( ) ). ‣ 4.3 Convergence with Stochastic Feedback ‣ 4 Analysis ‣ A Policy-Gradient Approach to Solving Imperfect-Information Games with Best-Iterate Convergence")
are met. (ii). By
[Lemma 4.3](#S4.Thmtheorem3 "Lemma 4.3 (Generalization of Proposition 1 in ( ) ). ‣ 4.3 Convergence with Stochastic Feedback ‣ 4 Analysis ‣ A Policy-Gradient Approach to Solving Imperfect-Information Games with Best-Iterate Convergence")
and union bound, we can extend the result of full information feedback
to the stochastic feedback. (iii). To ensure the coefficient for the
cumulated distance to NE after telescoping is still positive, we need to
bound the largest gap between the timesteps of two consecutive visits of
any infoset.

For any infoset $`s\in{\mathcal{S}}`$, we define
$`T_{s}\coloneqq\left\{t_{s}^{1},t_{s}^{2},\cdots\right\}`$, where each
$`t_{s}^{k}\in[T]`$ is the timestep that $`s`$ is along the sampled
trajectory. Then, we will show
[Algorithm 1](#alg1 "In 3 Q-Function based Regret Minimization (QFR) ‣ A Policy-Gradient Approach to Solving Imperfect-Information Games with Best-Iterate Convergence")
uses unbiased estimators so that we can derive an upper-bound by
[Lemma 4.3](#S4.Thmtheorem3 "Lemma 4.3 (Generalization of Proposition 1 in ( ) ). ‣ 4.3 Convergence with Stochastic Feedback ‣ 4 Analysis ‣ A Policy-Gradient Approach to Solving Imperfect-Information Games with Best-Iterate Convergence").
Note that for any
$`\bm{u}\in\Delta^{|\mathcal{A}_{s}|}_{\gamma_{s},\bm{\nu}_{s}}`$, the
expectation of the additional regularizer term is,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\Pr\left(t\in T_{s}\right)\left(\frac{\tau}{\mu_{p(s)}^{(t)}(\sigma(s))}\psi_{s}^{\Delta}(\bm{u})\right)=`$ | $`\displaystyle\mu_{p(s)}^{(t)}(\sigma(s))\mu_{-p(s)}^{(t)}(s)\left(\frac{\tau}{\mu_{p(s)}^{(t)}(\sigma(s))}\psi_{s}^{\Delta}(\bm{u})\right)`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle=`$ | $`\displaystyle\tau\mu_{-p(s)}^{(t)}(s)\psi_{s}^{\Delta}(\bm{u}).`$ |  |

Let $`s(h)`$ denote the infoset that the node $`h`$ is in. For the
original utility, suppose $`p(s)=1`$ without loss of generality, the
expectation of $`\widetilde{q}^{(t)}(s,a)`$ for any
$`a\in\mathcal{A}_{s}`$ is,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\frac{1}{\pi^{(t)}_{1}(a{\,|\,}s)}\sum_{h^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}\colon\exists h\in s,(h,a)\sqsubseteq h^{\prime}}\mu_{1}^{(t)}(\sigma_{1}(h^{\prime}))\mu_{2}^{(t)}(\sigma_{2}(h^{\prime}))\mu_{c}(h^{\prime})\mathcal{U}_{1}(h^{\prime})`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\pi^{(t)}_{1}(a{\,|\,}s)}\sum_{h^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}_{1}\colon\exists h\in s,(h,a)\sqsubseteq h^{\prime}}\mu_{1}^{(t)}(\sigma_{1}(h^{\prime}))\mu_{2}^{(t)}(\sigma_{2}(h^{\prime}))\mu_{c}(h^{\prime})\psi^{\Delta}_{s(h)}(\pi_{1}^{(t)})`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\pi^{(t)}_{1}(a{\,|\,}s)}\sum_{h^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}_{2}\colon\exists h\in s,(h,a)\sqsubseteq h^{\prime}}\mu_{1}^{(t)}(\sigma_{1}(h^{\prime}))\mu_{2}^{(t)}(\sigma_{2}(h^{\prime}))\mu_{c}(h^{\prime})\psi^{\Delta}_{s(h)}(\pi_{2}^{(t)}),`$ |  |

which is equal to $`q^{(t)}(s,a)`$ by definition.

By Lemma
[4.3](#S4.Thmtheorem3 "Lemma 4.3 (Generalization of Proposition 1 in ( ) ). ‣ 4.3 Convergence with Stochastic Feedback ‣ 4 Analysis ‣ A Policy-Gradient Approach to Solving Imperfect-Information Games with Best-Iterate Convergence")
and union bound, with probability at least $`1-\delta`$, the following
is satisfied for all infosets $`s\in{\mathcal{S}}`$,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\Pr\left(t\in T_{s}\right)\left(\frac{\tau}{\mu_{p(s)}^{(t)}(\sigma(s))}\psi_{s}^{\Delta}(\bm{u})\right)=`$ | $`\displaystyle\mu_{p(s)}^{(t)}(\sigma(s))\mu_{-p(s)}^{(t)}(s)\left(\frac{\tau}{\mu_{p(s)}^{(t)}(\sigma(s))}\psi_{s}^{\Delta}(\bm{u})\right)=\tau\mu_{-p(s)}^{(t)}(s)\psi_{s}^{\Delta}(\bm{u}).`$ |  |

where $`\psi^{\max}`$ is the upperbound of $`\psi_{s}^{\Delta}`$, which
is $`\max_{s\in{\mathcal{S}}}\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2}`$ when it is Euclidean
norm and $`\max_{s\in{\mathcal{S}}}\alpha_{s}\log|\mathcal{A}_{s}|`$
when it is entropy.

Let’s define $`t_{s}^{0}=1`$ for notational simplicity. Similar to the
proof of
[Theorem 4.2](#S4.Thmtheorem2 "Theorem 4.2 (Informal). ‣ 4.2 Convergence with Full Information Feedback ‣ 4 Analysis ‣ A Policy-Gradient Approach to Solving Imperfect-Information Games with Best-Iterate Convergence"),
for each $`k\leq|T_{s}|-1`$, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\eta_{s}\tau\psi_{s}^{\Delta}(\pi^{(t_{s}^{k})}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))-\eta_{s}\tau\psi_{s}^{\Delta}(\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))+\eta_{s}\left\langle-\widetilde{q}^{(t_{s}^{k})}(s,\cdot),\pi^{(t_{s}^{k})}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)-\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)\right\rangle`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\leq`$ | $`\displaystyle D_{\psi_{s}^{\Delta}}(\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s),\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t_{s}^{k})}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))-(1+\eta_{s}\tau)D_{\psi_{s}^{\Delta}}(\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s),\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t_{s}^{k+1})}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle-(1+\eta_{s}\tau)D_{\psi_{s}^{\Delta}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t_{s}^{k+1})}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s),\pi^{(t_{s}^{k})}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+\eta_{s}\left\langle\widetilde{q}^{(t_{s}^{k-1})}(s,\cdot)-\widetilde{q}^{(t_{s}^{k})}(s,\cdot),\pi^{(t_{s}^{k})}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t_{s}^{k+1})}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)\right\rangle.`$ |  |

By multiplying
$`m_{s}^{(t_{s}^{k})}=\frac{1}{\mu_{p(s)}^{(t_{s}^{k})}(\sigma(s))}`$ on
both sides and telescoping, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{|T_{s}|-1}\Big(\frac{\tau}{\mu_{p(s)}^{(t_{s}^{k})}(\sigma(s))}\left(\psi_{s}^{\Delta}(\pi^{(t_{s}^{k})}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))-\psi_{s}^{\Delta}(\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))\right)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\mu_{p(s)}^{(t_{s}^{k})}(\sigma(s))}\langle-\widetilde{q}^{(t_{s}^{k})}(s,\cdot),\pi^{(t_{s}^{k})}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)-\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)\rangle\Big)`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\leq`$ | $`\displaystyle\sum_{k=2}^{|T_{s}|}\left(\frac{1}{\mu_{p(s)}^{(t_{s}^{k})}(\sigma(s))}-\frac{1}{\mu_{p(s)}^{(t_{s}^{k-1})}(\sigma(s))}-\frac{\eta_{s}\tau}{\mu_{p(s)}^{(t_{s}^{k-1})}(\sigma(s))}\right)D_{\psi_{s}^{\Delta}}(\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s),\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t_{s}^{k})}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+2C_{s}^{\mathrm{diff}}\left\|\bm{q}\right\|_{\infty}\eta_{s}M_{2}|T_{s}|+\frac{1}{\mu_{p(s)}^{(t_{s}^{1})}(\sigma(s))}D_{\psi_{s}^{\Delta}}(\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s),\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t_{s}^{1})}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)).`$ |  |

Since the probability of visiting infoset $`s`$ at timestep $`t`$ is at
least $`\gamma^{2}\sum_{h\in s}\mu_{c}(h)`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\Pr\left(|t_{s}^{k}-t_{s}^{k-1}|>K_{s}\right)\leq(1-\gamma^{2}\sum_{h\in s}\mu_{c}(h))^{K_{s}}\leq\exp(-\gamma^{2}\sum_{h\in s}\mu_{c}(h)K_{s}).`$ |  |

Therefore, with probability $`1-\delta`$, all infosets
$`s\in{\mathcal{S}}`$ satisfies that for any $`2\leq k\leq|T_{s}|`$,
$`|t_{s}^{k}-t_{s}^{k-1}|\leq\frac{\log T+\log|{\mathcal{S}}|+\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{\gamma^{2}\sum_{h\in s}\mu_{c}(h)}\eqqcolon K_{s}`$.
Then,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mu_{p(s)}^{(t_{s}^{k})}(\sigma(s))}-\frac{1}{\mu_{p(s)}^{(t_{s}^{k-1})}(\sigma(s))}-\frac{\eta_{s}\tau}{\mu_{p(s)}^{(t_{s}^{k-1})}(\sigma(s))}\leq`$ | $`\displaystyle C_{s}^{-}\eta_{s}^{\rm anc}\frac{\log T+\log|{\mathcal{S}}|+\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{\gamma^{2}\sum_{h\in s}\mu_{c}(h)}-\eta_{s}\tau`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle=`$ | $`\displaystyle C_{s}^{-}\eta_{s}^{\rm anc}\frac{\log T+\log|{\mathcal{S}}|+\log\frac{1}{\delta}}{\gamma^{2}\sum_{h\in s}\mu_{c}(h)}-\eta_{s}\tau.`$ |  |

Therefore, when
$`\frac{\eta_{s}^{\rm anc}}{\eta_{s}}\leq\frac{\tau\gamma^{2}\sum_{h\in s}\mu_{c}(h)}{2C_{s}^{-}\left(\log T+\log|{\mathcal{S}}|+\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right)}`$,
the inequality above is upper-bounded by $`-\frac{\eta_{s}\tau}{2}`$.
Moreover, we can write it as (let $`t_{s}^{|T_{s}|+1}=T+1`$ for
notational simplicity),

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\sum_{k=2}^{|T_{s}|}\left(\frac{1}{\mu_{p(s)}^{(t_{s}^{k})}(\sigma(s))}-\frac{1}{\mu_{p(s)}^{(t_{s}^{k-1})}(\sigma(s))}-\frac{\eta_{s}\tau}{\mu_{p(s)}^{(t_{s}^{k-1})}(\sigma(s))}\right)D_{\psi_{s}^{\Delta}}(\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s),\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t_{s}^{k})}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\leq`$ | $`\displaystyle-\sum_{k=2}^{|T_{s}|}\sum_{t=t_{s}^{(k)}}^{t_{s}^{(k+1)}-1}\frac{\eta_{s}\tau}{2K_{s}}D_{\psi_{s}^{\Delta}}(\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s),\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t_{s}^{k})}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle=`$ | $`\displaystyle-\frac{\eta_{s}\tau\gamma^{2}\sum_{h\in s}\mu_{c}(h)}{2\left(\log T+\log|{\mathcal{S}}|+\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right)}\sum_{k=2}^{|T_{s}|}\sum_{t=t_{s}^{(k)}}^{t_{s}^{(k+1)}-1}D_{\psi_{s}^{\Delta}}(\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s),\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t_{s}^{k})}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{=}`$ | $`\displaystyle-\frac{\eta_{s}\tau\gamma^{2}\sum_{h\in s}\mu_{c}(h)}{2\left(\log T+\log|{\mathcal{S}}|+\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right)}\sum_{t=t_{s}^{(2)}}^{T}D_{\psi_{s}^{\Delta}}(\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s),\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\leq`$ | $`\displaystyle-\frac{\eta_{s}\tau\gamma^{2}\sum_{h\in s}\mu_{c}(h)}{2\left(\log T+\log|{\mathcal{S}}|+\log\frac{1}{\delta}\right)}\sum_{t=2}^{T}D_{\psi_{s}^{\Delta}}(\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s),\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))+2K_{s}\max_{\bm{x},\bm{y}\in\Delta_{\gamma_{s},\bm{\nu}_{s}}^{|\mathcal{A}_{s}|}}D_{\psi_{s}^{\Delta}}(\bm{x},\bm{y}).`$ |  |

$`(i)`$ uses the fact that for any $`t\in[t_{s}^{k},t_{s}^{k+1}-1]`$,
$`\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{p(s)}^{(t)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)=\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{p(s)}^{(t_{s}^{k})}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)`$.

Then, following rest of the proof for
[Theorem 4.2](#S4.Thmtheorem2 "Theorem 4.2 (Informal). ‣ 4.2 Convergence with Full Information Feedback ‣ 4 Analysis ‣ A Policy-Gradient Approach to Solving Imperfect-Information Games with Best-Iterate Convergence"),
we finish the proof. ∎

### F.1 Proof of [Lemma 4.3](#S4.Thmtheorem3 "Lemma 4.3 (Generalization of Proposition 1 in ( ) ). ‣ 4.3 Convergence with Stochastic Feedback ‣ 4 Analysis ‣ A Policy-Gradient Approach to Solving Imperfect-Information Games with Best-Iterate Convergence")

Let
$`d^{(t)}(\bm{u})\coloneqq\left(f^{(t)}(\bm{u})-f^{(t)}(\bm{u}^{(t)})\right)-\left(\widetilde{f}^{(t)}(\bm{u})-\widetilde{f}^{(t)}(\bm{u}^{(t)})\right)`$.
By the property of $`f^{(t)}`$, since $`\bm{u}^{(t)}`$ is
deterministically influenced by
$`\widetilde{f}^{(1)},\widetilde{f}^{(2)},\cdots,\widetilde{f}^{(t-1)}`$,
$`\mathbb{E}\left[\widetilde{f}^{(t)}(\bm{u}^{(t)}){\,|\,}\widetilde{f}^{(1)},\widetilde{f}^{(2)},\cdots,\widetilde{f}^{(t-1)}\right]=f^{(t)}(\bm{u}^{(t)})`$.
Moreover,
$`\mathbb{E}\Big[\widetilde{f}^{(t)}(\bm{u})\allowbreak{\,|\,}\widetilde{f}^{(1)},\widetilde{f}^{(2)},\cdots,\widetilde{f}^{(t-1)}\Big]=f^{(t)}(\bm{u})`$
for any fixed $`\bm{u}\in\mathcal{C}`$. Therefore, $`d^{(t)}(\bm{u})`$
is a martingale difference sequence. Then, we can apply the
Azuma-Hoeffding inequality in the following.

###### Lemma F.2 (Azuma-Hoeffding inequality).

For any martingale difference sequence
$`x^{(1)},x^{(2)},\cdots,x^{(T)}`$ with $`x^{(t)}\in[a^{(t)},b^{(t)}]`$,
we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\Pr\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}x^{(t)}\geq w\right)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{2w^{2}}{\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(b^{(t)}-a^{(t)}\right)^{2}}\right).`$ |  |

Note that $`\left|d^{(t)}(\bm{u})\right|\leq M+\widetilde{M}`$. By
applying
[Lemma F.2](#A6.Thmtheorem2 "Lemma F.2 (Azuma-Hoeffding inequality). ‣ F.1 Proof of ‣ Appendix F Proof of Theorem ‣ A Policy-Gradient Approach to Solving Imperfect-Information Games with Best-Iterate Convergence"),
we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\Pr\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}d^{(t)}(\bm{u})\geq w\right)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{2w^{2}}{\sum_{t=1}^{T}4(M+\widetilde{M})^{2}}\right).`$ |  |

Therefore, when taking
$`w=(M+\widetilde{M})\sqrt{2T\log\frac{1}{\delta}}`$, with probability
at least $`1-\delta`$,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(f^{(t)}(\bm{u})-f^{(t)}(\bm{u}^{(t)})\right)\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\widetilde{f}^{(t)}(\bm{u})-\widetilde{f}^{(t)}(\bm{u}^{(t)})\right)+(M+\widetilde{M})\sqrt{2T\log\frac{1}{\delta}}.`$ |  | ∎ |

## Appendix G Auxiliary Lemmas

In this section, we present the auxiliary lemmas for the theorems proved
in the previous part.

### G.1 Upperbound of Feedback

###### Lemma G.1 (Upperbound of Feedback $`q^{(t)}(s,\cdot)`$).

Consider the update-rule
([4.2](#S4.E2 "Equation 4.2 ‣ 4.2 Convergence with Full Information Feedback ‣ 4 Analysis ‣ A Policy-Gradient Approach to Solving Imperfect-Information Games with Best-Iterate Convergence")).
For any timestep $`t\in[T]`$, we have the following upper-bound on
$`q^{(t)}(s,\cdot)`$ and its unbiased estimator
$`\widetilde{q}^{(t)}(s,\cdot)`$, no matter whether it is counterfactual
value, trajectory Q-value, or Q-value.

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\left\|\bm{q}\right\|_{\infty}\coloneqq\begin{cases}\frac{\frac{\tau}{M_{1}}\left\|\bm{\alpha}\right\|_{\infty}\mathcal{D}\psi^{\max}+1}{\min_{s\in{\mathcal{S}},a\in\mathcal{A}_{s}}\gamma_{s}\nu_{s,a}}&\text{Outcome Sampling of Trajectory Q-value}\\
\frac{\tau}{M_{1}}\left\|\bm{\alpha}\right\|_{\infty}\mathcal{D}\psi^{\max}+1&\text{Otherwise}\end{cases}`$ |  | (G.1) |

where $`\mathcal{D}\coloneqq\max_{h\in\mathcal{H}}\mathcal{D}(h)`$ is
the maximum depth of infoset and $`\psi^{\max}`$ is the maximum of the
regularizer, which is
$`\frac{1}{2\min_{s\in{\mathcal{S}}}|\mathcal{A}_{s}|}`$ for Euclidean
distance and $`\max_{s\in{\mathcal{S}}}\log|\mathcal{A}_{s}|`$ for
entropy.

###### Proof.

When calculating the feedback $`\widetilde{q}^{(t)}(s,a)`$ for outcome
sampling of trajectory Q-value, we need to divide the probability of
choosing action $`a`$, which is
$`\pi^{(t)}(a{\,|\,}s)\geq\min_{s\in{\mathcal{S}},a\in\mathcal{A}_{s}}\gamma_{s}\nu_{s,a}`$.
Then, its upperbound is that of the full-information feedback setting
divided by the constant
$`\min_{s\in{\mathcal{S}},a\in\mathcal{A}_{s}}\gamma_{s}\nu_{s,a}`$.
Therefore, in the following, we will focus on the upperbound of
$`q^{(t)}(s,\cdot)`$ in the full-information feedback setting.

In the following proof, we only consider $`s\in{\mathcal{S}}_{1}`$ since
player $`1,2`$ are symmetric. Furthermore, we only need to prove the
upper-bound above when $`q^{(t)}(s,\cdot)`$ is Q-value, since by
definition, the Q-value
$`Q_{i}^{\bm{\pi}}(s,a)=\frac{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muQ\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{i}^{\bm{\pi}}(s,a)}{\sum_{h\in s}\mu_{c}(h)\mu_{1}^{\pi_{1}}(\sigma_{1}(h))\mu_{2}^{\pi_{2}}(\sigma_{2}(h))}\geq\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5muQ\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{i}^{\bm{\pi}}(s,a)`$
(similarly, it is also larger than the counterfactual value).

Let $`s(h)`$ be the infoset that node $`h`$ is in. Firstly, when
$`\tau=0`$, which means only considering the contribution of
$`\mathcal{U}_{1}`$ to $`q^{(t)}(s,\cdot)`$, for every
$`s\in{\mathcal{S}}_{1}`$, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\left|Q_{i}^{\bm{\pi}}(s,a)\right|`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle=`$ | $`\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sum_{h\in s}\mu_{c}(h)\mu_{1}^{\pi_{1}}(\sigma_{1}(h))\mu_{2}^{\pi_{2}}(\sigma_{2}(h))}\left|\sum_{h^{\prime}\colon\exists h\in s,(h,a)\sqsubseteq h^{\prime}}\mu_{c}(h^{\prime})\mathcal{U}_{1}(h^{\prime})\mu_{1}^{\pi_{1}}(\sigma_{1}(h^{\prime}))\mu_{2}^{\pi_{2}}(\sigma_{2}(h^{\prime}))\right|`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{=}`$ | $`\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sum_{h\in s}\mu_{c}(h)\mu_{1}^{\pi_{1}}(\sigma_{1}(h))\mu_{2}^{\pi_{2}}(\sigma_{2}(h))}\left|\sum_{h^{\prime}\colon\exists h\in s,(h,a)\sqsubseteq h^{\prime},\mathcal{A}_{s(h^{\prime})}=\emptyset}\mu_{c}(h^{\prime})\mathcal{U}_{1}(h^{\prime})\mu_{1}^{\pi_{1}}(\sigma_{1}(h^{\prime}))\mu_{2}^{\pi_{2}}(\sigma_{2}(h^{\prime}))\right|`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\leq`$ | $`\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sum_{h\in s}\mu_{c}(h)\mu_{1}^{\pi_{1}}(\sigma_{1}(h))\mu_{2}^{\pi_{2}}(\sigma_{2}(h))}\sum_{h^{\prime}\colon\exists h\in s,(h,a)\sqsubseteq h^{\prime},\mathcal{A}_{s(h^{\prime})}=\emptyset}\mu_{c}(h^{\prime})\mu_{1}^{\pi_{1}}(\sigma_{1}(h^{\prime}))\mu_{2}^{\pi_{2}}(\sigma_{2}(h^{\prime}))`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{=}`$ | $`\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sum_{h\in s}\mu_{c}(h)\mu_{1}^{\pi_{1}}(\sigma_{1}(h))\mu_{2}^{\pi_{2}}(\sigma_{2}(h))}\sum_{h\in s}\mu_{c}(h)\mu_{1}^{\pi_{1}}(\sigma_{1}(h))\mu_{2}^{\pi_{2}}(\sigma_{2}(h))=1.`$ |  |

$`(i)`$ is because $`\mathcal{U}_{1}(h)\not=0`$ only if $`h`$ is a
terminal node. $`(ii)`$ is by the tree structure of EFG. Now consider
$`\tau>0`$ and $`\mathcal{U}_{1}(h)\equiv 0`$ for any
$`h\in\mathcal{H}`$. Moreover, we will only show the upperbound when
using dilated regularizer, since bidilated regularizer is upperbounded
by the dilated one.

Let
$`S^{(t)}(s)=\left\langle q^{(t)}(s,\cdot),\pi^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)\right\rangle-\frac{\tau}{m^{(t)}_{s}}\psi_{s}^{\Delta}(\pi^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))`$
when $`\mathcal{A}_{s}\not=\emptyset`$ ($`s`$ is not terminal node) and
$`S^{(t)}(s)=0`$ when $`\mathcal{A}_{s}=\emptyset`$ ($`s`$ is the
terminal node).

We will prove
$`\left|S^{(t)}(s)\right|\leq\frac{\tau}{M_{1}}\left\|\bm{\alpha}\right\|_{\infty}\left(\mathcal{D}-\mathcal{D}(s)\right)\psi^{\max}`$
by induction. For infoset $`s\in{\mathcal{S}}_{1}`$ with
$`\mathcal{D}(s)=D`$, we have
$`S^{(t)}(s)=0=\tau\left\|\bm{\alpha}\right\|_{\infty}\left(\mathcal{D}-\mathcal{D}(s)\right)\psi^{\max}`$.
Therefore, the initial step of induction is completed.

Consider when all $`s^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{1}`$ with
$`\mathcal{D}(s^{\prime})>d`$ for some constant $`d`$,
$`S^{(t)}(s^{\prime})\leq\frac{\tau}{M_{1}}\left\|\bm{\alpha}\right\|_{\infty}\left(\mathcal{D}-\mathcal{D}(s^{\prime})\right)\psi^{\max}`$.
Let $`\Pr(h\to h^{\prime})`$ be the probability of reaching
$`h^{\prime}`$ from node $`h`$, when considering all nodes encountered
along the path (player $`1,2`$ action node and the chance node) for
notational simplicity. Then, for infoset $`s\in{\mathcal{S}}_{1}`$ with
$`\mathcal{D}(s)=d`$, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\left|Q_{i}^{\bm{\pi}}(s,a)\right|`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle=`$ | $`\displaystyle\left|\sum_{s^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{1}\colon\sigma(s^{\prime})=(s,a)}S^{(t)}(s^{\prime})\sum_{h\in s}\frac{\mu_{c}(h)\mu_{1}^{\pi_{1}}(\sigma_{1}(h))\mu_{2}^{\pi_{2}}(\sigma_{2}(h))}{\sum_{h^{\prime}\in s}\mu_{c}(h^{\prime})\mu_{1}^{\pi_{1}}(\sigma_{1}(h^{\prime}))\mu_{2}^{\pi_{2}}(\sigma_{2}(h^{\prime}))}\sum_{h^{\prime}\in s^{\prime}}\Pr(h\to h^{\prime})\right|`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\leq`$ | $`\displaystyle\sum_{s^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{S}}_{1}\colon\sigma(s^{\prime})=(s,a)}\frac{\tau}{M_{1}}\left\|\bm{\alpha}\right\|_{\infty}\left(\mathcal{D}-\mathcal{D}(s)-1\right)\psi^{\max}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\cdot\sum_{h\in s}\frac{\mu_{c}(h)\mu_{1}^{\pi_{1}}(\sigma_{1}(h))\mu_{2}^{\pi_{2}}(\sigma_{2}(h))}{\sum_{h^{\prime}\in s}\mu_{c}(h^{\prime})\mu_{1}^{\pi_{1}}(\sigma_{1}(h^{\prime}))\mu_{2}^{\pi_{2}}(\sigma_{2}(h^{\prime}))}\sum_{h^{\prime}\in s^{\prime}}\Pr(h\to h^{\prime})`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle=`$ | $`\displaystyle\frac{\tau}{M_{1}}\left\|\bm{\alpha}\right\|_{\infty}\left(\mathcal{D}-\mathcal{D}(s)-1\right)\psi^{\max}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\cdot\sum_{h\in s}\frac{\mu_{c}(h)\mu_{1}^{\pi_{1}}(\sigma_{1}(h))\mu_{2}^{\pi_{2}}(\sigma_{2}(h))}{\sum_{h^{\prime}\in s}\mu_{c}(h^{\prime})\mu_{1}^{\pi_{1}}(\sigma_{1}(h^{\prime}))\mu_{2}^{\pi_{2}}(\sigma_{2}(h^{\prime}))}\sum_{h^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}_{1}\colon\sigma_{1}(h^{\prime})=(s,a)}\Pr(h\to h^{\prime})`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle=`$ | $`\displaystyle\frac{\tau}{M_{1}}\left\|\bm{\alpha}\right\|_{\infty}\left(\mathcal{D}-\mathcal{D}(s)-1\right)\psi^{\max}.`$ |  |

Therefore,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\left|S^{(t)}(s)\right|=\left|\left\langle q^{(t)}(s,\cdot),\pi^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)\right\rangle-\frac{\tau}{m^{(t)}_{s}}\psi_{s}^{\Delta}(\pi^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))\right|`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\leq`$ | $`\displaystyle\frac{\tau}{M_{1}}\left\|\bm{\alpha}\right\|_{\infty}\left(\mathcal{D}-\mathcal{D}(s)-1\right)\psi^{\max}+\frac{\tau}{M_{1}}\left\|\bm{\alpha}\right\|_{\infty}\psi^{\max}`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle=`$ | $`\displaystyle\frac{\tau}{M_{1}}\left\|\bm{\alpha}\right\|_{\infty}\left(\mathcal{D}-\mathcal{D}(s)\right)\psi^{\max}.`$ |  |

This concludes the induction step.∎

### G.2 Bounding $`M_{1},M_{2}`$

By choosing $`\gamma_{s}`$ as a fixed constant $`\gamma_{0}>0`$ for any
player $`i\in[2]`$ and $`s\in{\mathcal{S}}_{i}`$. $`\nu_{s,a}`$ is
chosen to be proportional to the number of terminal infosets
($`s^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{S}}`$ with
$`\mathcal{A}_{s^{\prime}}=\emptyset`$) in the subtree rooted at
$`(s,a)`$, we can get
$`\gamma\geq\frac{\gamma_{0}^{\mathcal{D}}}{|{\mathcal{S}}|}`$. Then, we
have the following lowerbound and upperbound on $`m^{(t)}_{s}`$.

###### Lemma G.2.

When $`\mu_{1}^{(t)},\mu_{2}^{(t)}\succeq\gamma`$, $`m^{(t)}_{s}`$ are
lowerbounded by $`M_{1}`$ and upperbounded by $`M_{2}`$ with the
following $`M_{1},M_{2}`$ for different feedback.

|  |  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle M_{1}\coloneqq\begin{cases}\gamma\min_{s\in{\mathcal{S}}}\sum_{h\in s}\mu_{c}(h)&\text{Q-value}\\
1&\text{Trajectory Q-value}\\
1&\text{ Counterfactual~Value}\end{cases}`$ | $`\displaystyle M_{2}\coloneqq\begin{cases}1&\text{Q-value}\\
\frac{1}{\gamma}&\text{Trajectory Q-value}\\
1&\text{ Counterfactual Value}\end{cases}`$ |  | (G.2) |

###### Proof.

We only prove the lowerbound and upperbound for infoset
$`s\in{\mathcal{S}}_{1}`$ since two players are symmetric.

For counterfactual value, since $`m^{(t)}_{s}\equiv 1`$,
$`M_{1},M_{2}=1`$.

For trajectory Q-value, since
$`m^{(t)}_{s}=\frac{1}{\mu_{1}^{(t)}(\sigma(s))}`$, we have
$`M_{2}=\frac{1}{\gamma}\geq m^{(t)}_{s}\geq 1=M_{1}`$.

For Q-value,
$`m^{(t)}_{s}=\sum_{h\in s}\mu_{c}(h)\mu_{2}^{(t)}(\sigma_{2}(h))`$.
Since the reach probability
$`\mu_{1}^{\pi_{1}}(\sigma(s))\sum_{h\in s}\mu_{c}(h)\mu_{2}^{(t)}(\sigma_{2}(h))\leq 1`$
for any $`\pi_{1}`$, we can let $`\pi_{1}`$ play deterministically to
reach $`s`$. In this way, $`m^{(t)}_{s}`$ is equal to the reach
probability so it is also upperbounded by one. At the same time,
$`m^{(t)}_{s}\geq\gamma\sum_{h\in s}\mu_{c}(h)\geq\gamma\min_{s\in{\mathcal{S}}}\sum_{h\in s}\mu_{c}(h)`$.∎

### G.3 Proof of Lemma [C.2](#A3.Thmtheorem2 "Lemma C.2. ‣ Proof. ‣ Lemma C.1 (Stability of 𝑚^(𝑡)_𝑠 under Euclidean regularizer). ‣ C.1 Stability of Trajectory Q-value ‣ Appendix C Stability of Trajectory Q-value and Q-Value ‣ A Policy-Gradient Approach to Solving Imperfect-Information Games with Best-Iterate Convergence")

###### Lemma G.3.

Consider update-rule
([4.2](#S4.E2 "Equation 4.2 ‣ 4.2 Convergence with Full Information Feedback ‣ 4 Analysis ‣ A Policy-Gradient Approach to Solving Imperfect-Information Games with Best-Iterate Convergence")).
When
$`\psi^{\Delta}_{s}(\bm{u})=\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2}\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{s}}u_{a}^{2}`$
is the Euclidean distance where $`\alpha_{s}>0`$ is a constant, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle C_{s}^{\text{diff}}=\frac{|\mathcal{A}_{s}|}{\alpha_{s}}\left\|\bm{q}\right\|_{\infty}+\frac{\tau}{M_{1}}\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{s}|}.`$ |  | (G.3) |

Let
$`\tau_{s}^{(t)}\coloneq\frac{\tau\mu_{-p(s)}^{(t)}(s)}{m_{s}^{(t)}}`$.
When
$`\psi^{\Delta}_{s}(\bm{u})=\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2}\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{s}}u_{a}^{2}`$,
we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\left\|\pi^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)\right\|`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle=`$ | $`\displaystyle\left\|{\rm Proj}_{\Delta^{|\mathcal{A}_{s}|}_{\gamma_{s},\bm{\nu}_{s}}}\left(\frac{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)}{1+\eta_{s}\tau_{s}^{(t-1)}}+\frac{\eta_{s}}{\alpha_{s}(1+\eta_{s}\tau_{s}^{(t-1)})}q^{(t-1)}(s,\cdot)\right)-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)\right\|`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\leq`$ | $`\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)}{1+\eta_{s}\tau_{s}^{(t-1)}}+\frac{\eta_{s}}{\alpha_{s}(1+\eta_{s}\tau_{s}^{(t-1)})}q^{(t-1)}(s,\cdot)-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)\right\|`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\leq`$ | $`\displaystyle\eta_{s}\left(\frac{1}{\alpha_{s}(1+\eta_{s}\tau_{s}^{(t-1)})}\left\|q^{(t-1)}(s,\cdot)\right\|+\frac{\tau_{s}^{(t-1)}}{1+\eta_{s}\tau_{s}^{(t-1)}}\left\|\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)\right\|\right)`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\leq`$ | $`\displaystyle\eta_{s}\left(\frac{1}{\alpha_{s}}\left\|q^{(t-1)}(s,\cdot)\right\|+\tau_{s}^{(t-1)}\left\|\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)\right\|\right)`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\leq`$ | $`\displaystyle\eta_{s}\left(\frac{\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{s}|}}{\alpha_{s}}\left\|\bm{q}\right\|_{\infty}+\frac{\tau}{M_{1}}\right).`$ |  |

In the last line, we use the fact that $`\mu_{-p(s)}^{(t)}(s)\leq 1`$.

As a result,
$`\left\|\pi^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)\right\|_{1}\leq\eta_{s}\left(\frac{|\mathcal{A}_{s}|}{\alpha_{s}}\left\|\bm{q}\right\|_{\infty}+\frac{\tau}{M_{1}}\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{s}|}\right)`$.

Similarly,
$`\left\|\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t+1)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)\right\|_{1}\leq\eta_{s}\left(\frac{|\mathcal{A}_{s}|}{\alpha_{s}}\left\|\bm{q}\right\|_{\infty}+\frac{\tau}{M_{1}}\sqrt{|\mathcal{A}_{s}|}\right)`$.
∎

###### Proof of Lemma [C.5](#A3.Thmtheorem5 "Lemma C.5. ‣ Proof of Lemma . ‣ C.1 Stability of Trajectory Q-value ‣ Appendix C Stability of Trajectory Q-value and Q-Value ‣ A Policy-Gradient Approach to Solving Imperfect-Information Games with Best-Iterate Convergence").

Let
$`\tau_{s}^{(t)}\coloneq\frac{\tau\mu_{-p(s)}^{(t)}(s)}{m_{s}^{(t)}}`$.
When
$`\psi^{\Delta}_{s}(\bm{u})=\alpha_{s}\left(\log|\mathcal{A}_{s}|+\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{s}}u_{a}\log u_{a}\right)`$,
the update-rule
([4.2](#S4.E2 "Equation 4.2 ‣ 4.2 Convergence with Full Information Feedback ‣ 4 Analysis ‣ A Policy-Gradient Approach to Solving Imperfect-Information Games with Best-Iterate Convergence"))
is equivalent to

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\pi^{(t)}(a{\,|\,}s)`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle=`$ | $`\displaystyle\max\left\{\frac{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)}(a{\,|\,}s)^{\frac{1}{1+\eta_{s}\tau_{s}^{(t-1)}}}\exp\left(\frac{\eta_{s}}{\alpha_{s}\left(1+\eta_{s}\tau_{s}^{(t-1)}\right)}q^{(t)}(s,a)\right)}{Z},\gamma_{s}\nu_{s,a}\right\}`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle=`$ | $`\displaystyle\max\left\{\frac{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)}(a{\,|\,}s)\exp\left(\frac{\eta_{s}}{\alpha_{s}\left(1+\eta_{s}\tau_{s}^{(t-1)}\right)}q^{(t)}(s,a)-\frac{\eta_{s}\tau}{m^{(t-1)}_{s}\left(1+\eta_{s}\tau_{s}^{(t-1)}\right)}\log\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)}(a{\,|\,}s)\right)}{Z},\gamma_{s}\nu_{s,a}\right\}`$ |  |

for any $`a\in\mathcal{A}_{s}`$, where $`Z>0`$ is a normalizing constant
to ensure $`\pi^{(t)}(a{\,|\,}s)`$ is still a probability distribution
over $`\Delta^{|\mathcal{A}_{s}|}`$. The equivalency is proved in Lemma
[G.4](#A7.Thmtheorem4 "Lemma G.4. ‣ G.4 Update Rule of MWU ‣ Appendix G Auxiliary Lemmas ‣ A Policy-Gradient Approach to Solving Imperfect-Information Games with Best-Iterate Convergence").
For notational simplicity, we define
$`l^{(t)}(s,a)\coloneqq-\frac{1}{\alpha_{s}\left(1+\eta_{s}\tau_{s}^{(t-1)}\right)}q^{(t)}(s,a)+\frac{\tau}{m^{(t-1)}_{s}\left(1+\eta_{s}\tau_{s}^{(t-1)}\right)}\log\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)}(a{\,|\,}s)`$
so that
$`\pi^{(t)}(a{\,|\,}s)=\max\left\{\frac{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)}(a{\,|\,}s)\exp\left(-\eta_{s}l^{(t)}(s,a)\right)}{Z},\gamma_{s}\nu_{s,a}\right\}`$.

Firstly, for $`\gamma_{s}=1`$, we have
$`\pi^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)=\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)=\bm{\nu}_{s}`$.
Therefore, $`C_{s}^{\text{diff}}=0`$ and
$`\frac{\pi^{(t)}(a{\,|\,}s)}{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)}(a{\,|\,}s)}=1`$
for any $`a\in\mathcal{A}_{s}`$. In the following, we assume
$`\gamma_{s}<1`$.

We can see that $`\pi^{(t)}(a{\,|\,}s)`$ is monotonically decreasing
with respect to $`Z`$. When
$`Z<\exp\left(-\eta_{s}\max_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{s}}l^{(t)}(s,a^{\prime})\right)`$,
for any $`a\in\mathcal{A}_{s}`$, we have
$`\pi^{(t)}(a{\,|\,}s)\geq\frac{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)}(a{\,|\,}s)\exp\left(-\eta_{s}l^{(t)}(s,a)\right)}{Z}>\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)}(a{\,|\,}s)`$.
Then, $`\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{s}}\pi^{(t)}(a{\,|\,}s)>1`$.

Therefore,
$`Z\geq\exp\left(-\eta_{s}\max_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{s}}l^{(t)}(s,a^{\prime})\right)`$.

Similarly, when
$`Z>\exp\left(-\eta_{s}\min_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{s}}l^{(t)}(s,a^{\prime})\right)`$,
for any $`a\in\mathcal{A}_{s}`$, we have
$`\frac{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)}(a{\,|\,}s)\exp\left(-\eta_{s}l^{(t)}(s,a)\right)}{Z}<\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)}(a{\,|\,}s)`$.
It implies that
$`\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{s}}\pi^{(t)}(a{\,|\,}s)<\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{s}}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)}(a{\,|\,}s)`$,
unless
$`\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)}(a{\,|\,}s)=\gamma_{s}\nu_{s,a}`$
for all $`a\in\mathcal{A}_{s}`$, which is impossible since we assume
$`\gamma_{s}<1`$. Therefore,
$`Z\leq\exp\left(-\eta_{s}\min_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{s}}l^{(t)}(s,a^{\prime})\right)`$.

Then, if
$`\frac{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)}(a{\,|\,}s)\exp\left(-\eta_{s}l^{(t)}(s,a)\right)}{Z}\geq\gamma_{s}\nu_{s,a}`$,
we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle 1\leq`$ | $`\displaystyle\frac{\pi^{(t)}(a{\,|\,}s)}{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)}(a{\,|\,}s)}`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle=`$ | $`\displaystyle\frac{\exp\left(-\eta_{s}l^{(t)}(s,a)\right)}{Z}`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\leq`$ | $`\displaystyle\exp\left(\eta_{s}\max_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{s}}l^{(t)}(s,a^{\prime})-\eta_{s}l^{(t)}(s,a)\right)`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\leq`$ | $`\displaystyle\exp\left(\frac{\eta_{s}}{\alpha_{s}\left(1+\eta_{s}\tau_{s}^{(t-1)}\right)}\left(\max_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{s}}q^{(t)}(s,a^{\prime})-q^{(t)}(s,a)\right)+\frac{\eta_{s}\tau_{s}^{(t-1)}}{1+\eta_{s}\tau_{s}^{(t-1)}}\log\max_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{s}}\frac{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)}(a^{\prime}{\,|\,}s)}{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)}(a{\,|\,}s)}\right)`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\leq}`$ | $`\displaystyle\exp\left(\frac{\eta_{s}}{\alpha_{s}}\left(2\left\|\bm{q}\right\|_{\infty}+\frac{\tau\alpha_{s}}{M_{1}}\log\frac{1}{\gamma}\right)\right)`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{\leq}`$ | $`\displaystyle 1+2\frac{\eta_{s}}{\alpha_{s}}\left(2\left\|\bm{q}\right\|_{\infty}+\frac{\tau\alpha_{s}}{M_{1}}\log\frac{1}{\gamma}\right)`$ |  |

In $`(i)`$ we use the fact
$`\left\|\bm{q}\right\|_{\infty}\geq\left|q^{(t)}(s,a)\right|`$ for any
$`a\in\mathcal{A}_{s}`$. In $`(ii)`$ we use $`e^{x}\leq 1+2x`$ for
$`x\in[0,1]`$.

If
$`\frac{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)}(a{\,|\,}s)\exp\left(-\eta_{s}l^{(t)}(s,a)\right)}{Z}<\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)}(a{\,|\,}s)`$,
we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\frac{\exp\left(-\eta_{s}l^{(t)}(s,a)\right)}{Z}\geq`$ | $`\displaystyle\exp\left(\eta_{s}\min_{a^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{s}}l^{(t)}(s,a^{\prime})-\eta_{s}l^{(t)}(s,a)\right)`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\geq`$ | $`\displaystyle\exp\left(-\frac{\eta_{s}}{\alpha_{s}}\left(2\left\|\bm{q}\right\|_{\infty}+\frac{\tau\alpha_{s}}{M_{1}}\log\frac{1}{\gamma}\right)\right).`$ |  |

Therefore,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle 1\geq\frac{\pi^{(t)}(a{\,|\,}s)}{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)}(a{\,|\,}s)}\geq\frac{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)}(a{\,|\,}s)\exp\left(-\eta_{s}l^{(t)}(s,a)\right)}{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)}(a{\,|\,}s)Z}\geq`$ | $`\displaystyle\exp\left(-\frac{\eta_{s}}{\alpha_{s}}\left(2\left\|\bm{q}\right\|_{\infty}+\frac{\tau\alpha_{s}}{M_{1}}\log\frac{1}{\gamma}\right)\right)`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\geq`$ | $`\displaystyle 1-\frac{\eta_{s}}{\alpha_{s}}\left(2\left\|\bm{q}\right\|_{\infty}+\frac{\tau\alpha_{s}}{M_{1}}\log\frac{1}{\gamma}\right).`$ |  |

Then, for any $`a\in\mathcal{A}_{s}`$, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\left|\frac{\pi^{(t)}(a{\,|\,}s)}{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)}(a{\,|\,}s)}-1\right|\leq`$ | $`\displaystyle 2\frac{\eta_{s}}{\alpha_{s}}\left(2\left\|\bm{q}\right\|_{\infty}+\frac{\tau\alpha_{s}}{M_{1}}\log\frac{1}{\gamma}\right).`$ |  |

Therefore,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\left\|\pi^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)\right\|_{1}=\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{s}}\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)}(a{\,|\,}s)\left|\frac{\pi^{(t)}(a{\,|\,}s)}{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)}(a{\,|\,}s)}-1\right|\leq 2\frac{\eta_{s}}{\alpha_{s}}\left(2\left\|\bm{q}\right\|_{\infty}+\frac{\tau\alpha_{s}}{M_{1}}\log\frac{1}{\gamma}\right).
``` |  |

Similarly, the upperbound above also holds for
$`\left|\frac{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t+1)}(a{\,|\,}s)}{\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)}(a{\,|\,}s)}-1\right|`$.
∎

### G.4 Update Rule of MWU

For ease of representation, we ignore the learning rate $`\eta`$ without
loss of generality (the update-rule is the same without $`\eta`$ when
multiplying both the gradient $`\bm{g}`$ and $`\tau`$ by $`\eta`$).

###### Lemma G.4.

Consider the update-rule
$`\bm{x}^{(2)}=\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}}_{\bm{x}\in\Delta^{\gamma,\bm{\nu}}_{|\mathcal{A}|}}\left\langle\bm{x},\bm{g}\right\rangle+\tau\psi(\bm{x})+D_{\psi}(\bm{x},\bm{x}^{(1)})`$
where $`\bm{x}^{(1)}\in\Delta^{\gamma,\bm{\nu}}_{|\mathcal{A}|}`$,
$`\gamma\geq 0`$ is a constant, and
$`\bm{\nu}\in\Delta^{|\mathcal{A}|}`$, $`\mathcal{A}`$ is the action set
and $`\psi(\bm{x})=\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}x\log x+\log|\mathcal{A}|`$.
Then, the update-rule is equivalent to

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle x^{(2)}_{a}=\max\left\{\frac{\left(x^{(1)}_{a}\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\tau}}\exp(-\frac{g_{a}}{1+\tau})}{Z},\gamma\nu_{a}\right\}`$ |  | (G.4) |

for any $`a\in\mathcal{A}`$. $`Z>0`$ is the normalizing constant to
ensure $`\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}x^{(2)}_{a}=1`$.

###### Proof.

By definition of Bregman divergence,
$`D_{\psi}(\bm{x},\bm{x}^{(1)})=\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}x_{a}\log\frac{x_{a}}{x^{(1)}_{a}}`$.
Therefore, the Lagrangian function of the update-rule is

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(\bm{x},\alpha,\bm{\beta})\coloneqq\left\langle\bm{x},\bm{g}\right\rangle+\tau\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}x_{a}\log x_{a}+\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}x_{a}\log\frac{x_{a}}{x^{(1)}_{a}}+\alpha(\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}x_{a}-1)+\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}}\beta_{a}(x_{a}-\gamma\nu_{a}).`$ |  |

By taking $`\nabla_{\bm{x}}\mathcal{F}(\bm{x},\alpha,\bm{\lambda})=0`$,
for any $`a\in\mathcal{A}`$, we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle g_{a}+\tau\log x^{(2)}_{a}+\log\frac{x^{(2)}_{a}}{x^{(1)}_{a}}+1+\tau+\alpha+\beta_{a}=0,`$ |  |

which implies that

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle x^{(2)}_{a}=\left(x^{(1)}_{a}\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\tau}}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{1+\tau}\left(g_{a}+1+\tau+\alpha+\beta_{a}\right)\right).`$ |  |

By duality, $`\beta_{a}\leq 0`$. By complementary slackness, we have
$`\beta_{a}(x^{(2)}_{a}-\gamma\nu_{a})=0`$. Therefore, when
$`\beta_{a}<0`$, we have $`x^{(2)}_{a}=\gamma\nu_{a}`$, which implies
that
$`\left(x^{(1)}_{a}\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\tau}}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{1+\tau}\left(g_{a}+1+\tau+\alpha\right)\right)<\gamma\nu_{a}`$.
When $`\beta_{a}=0`$, we have $`x^{(2)}_{a}\geq\gamma\nu_{a}`$ so that
$`\left(x^{(1)}_{a}\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\tau}}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{1+\tau}\left(g_{a}+1+\tau+\alpha\right)\right)\geq\gamma\nu_{a}`$.
Therefore, the effect of $`\beta_{a}`$ is equivalent to take a max on
$`\left(x^{(1)}_{a}\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\tau}}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{1+\tau}\left(g_{a}+1+\tau+\alpha\right)\right)`$
and we have the update-rule

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle x^{(2)}_{a}=`$ | $`\displaystyle\max\left\{\left(x^{(1)}_{a}\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\tau}}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{1+\tau}\left(g_{a}+1+\tau+\alpha\right)\right),\gamma\nu_{a}\right\}`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle=`$ | $`\displaystyle\max\left\{\frac{\left(x^{(1)}_{a}\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\tau}}\exp(-\frac{g_{a}}{1+\tau})}{Z},\gamma\nu_{a}\right\}`$ |  |

where $`Z=\exp\left(\frac{1+\tau+\alpha}{1+\tau}\right)`$.∎

###### Remark G.5.

In practice, we can implement the update-rule in Lemma
[G.4](#A7.Thmtheorem4 "Lemma G.4. ‣ G.4 Update Rule of MWU ‣ Appendix G Auxiliary Lemmas ‣ A Policy-Gradient Approach to Solving Imperfect-Information Games with Best-Iterate Convergence")
as follows. We assume $`\gamma<1`$ since otherwise we can simply let
$`\bm{x}^{(2)}=\bm{\nu}`$.

- •
  Compute
  $`\widehat{x}_{a}=\left(x^{(1)}_{a}\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\tau}}\exp(-\frac{g_{a}}{1+\tau})`$
  and sort it in increasing order, which is
  $`\widehat{x}_{1}\leq\widehat{x}_{2}\leq\cdots\leq\widehat{x}_{|\mathcal{A}|}`$.
  Simultaneously, adjusting $`\bm{\nu}`$ according to the sorting of
  $`\widehat{\bm{x}}`$ to get $`\widehat{\bm{\nu}}`$, which is the
  lowerbound $`\widehat{\bm{x}}`$ should satisfy.
- •
  Enumerate $`i=0,1,2,\cdots,|\mathcal{A}|`$. Let
  $`Z=\frac{\sum_{j>i}\widehat{x}_{j}}{1-\gamma\sum_{j=1}^{i}\widehat{\nu}_{j}}`$.
- •
  Check $`\widehat{x}_{i}\leq\gamma\widehat{\nu}_{i}Z`$ if $`i>0`$ and
  $`\widehat{x}_{i+1}\geq\gamma\widehat{\nu}_{i+1}Z`$ if
  $`i<|\mathcal{A}|`$. If the current $`Z`$ satisfies, return it.
  Otherwise, continue the enumeration.

According to the monotonicity of
$`\max\left\{\frac{\left(x^{(1)}_{a}\right)^{\frac{1}{1+\tau}}\exp(-\frac{g_{a}}{1+\tau})}{Z},\gamma\nu_{a}\right\}`$
with respect to $`Z`$, the algorithm above will definitely find the
correct $`Z`$ and the time complexity is
$`O(|\mathcal{A}|\log|\mathcal{A}|)`$ (the bottleneck is the sort).

## Appendix H Experiment Details

[Figure 1](#S5.F1 "In 5 Experiments ‣ A Policy-Gradient Approach to Solving Imperfect-Information Games with Best-Iterate Convergence")
and
[Figure 2](#A8.F2 "In Appendix H Experiment Details ‣ A Policy-Gradient Approach to Solving Imperfect-Information Games with Best-Iterate Convergence")
are conducted on 240 cores of Intel Xeon Platinum 8260 and
[Figure 3](#A8.F3 "In Appendix H Experiment Details ‣ A Policy-Gradient Approach to Solving Imperfect-Information Games with Best-Iterate Convergence")
is conducted on Intel(R) Xeon Gold 6248 with NVidia Volta V100. The code
is based on LiteEFG \[[Liu et al., 2024](#bib.bib32)\] with game
environments implemented by OpenSpiel \[[Lanctot et al.,
2019](#bib.bib29)\]. The range of grid search and the best
hyper-parameters can be found in the supplementary materials.

We present the experimental results for full information feedback in
[Figure 2](#A8.F2 "In Appendix H Experiment Details ‣ A Policy-Gradient Approach to Solving Imperfect-Information Games with Best-Iterate Convergence"),
that is, we use trajectory Q-value, Q-value, or counterfactual value as
$`q^{(t)}(s,\cdot)`$ in the update rule
([4.2](#S4.E2 "Equation 4.2 ‣ 4.2 Convergence with Full Information Feedback ‣ 4 Analysis ‣ A Policy-Gradient Approach to Solving Imperfect-Information Games with Best-Iterate Convergence")).
In
Figure [2](#A8.F2 "Figure 2 ‣ Appendix H Experiment Details ‣ A Policy-Gradient Approach to Solving Imperfect-Information Games with Best-Iterate Convergence"),
we plot the last-iterate performance for all the algorithms to ensure
the comparison is fair.

Figure 2: The result of full-information feedback in four benchmark
games. We compare with CFR \[[Zinkevich et al., 2007](#bib.bib51)\],
CFR+ \[[Tammelin et al., 2015](#bib.bib48)\], MMD \[[Sokota et al.,
2023](#bib.bib44)\], DCFR \[[Brown and Sandholm, 2019](#bib.bib5)\], and
PCFR+ \[[Farina et al., 2021a](#bib.bib14)\]. We can see that QFR
outperforms MMD in all games. However, due to multiplicative noise
caused by using Q-values, QFR cannot outperform PCFR+, an advanced
variant of CFR.

In
[Figure 3](#A8.F3 "In Appendix H Experiment Details ‣ A Policy-Gradient Approach to Solving Imperfect-Information Games with Best-Iterate Convergence"),
we present the ablation study on importance sampling when the strategy
is approximated by the neural network. Our implementation of QFR is
based on PPO \[[Schulman et al., 2017](#bib.bib42)\] in CleanRL \[[Huang
et al., 2022](#bib.bib22)\]. We can see that with importance sampling,
the network gradient blows up so that the network does not converge,
even though we have applied gradient clipping.

Figure 3: The result of QFR with sampling feedback. We can see that with
importance sampling, the gradient norm keeps growing so that the network
does not converge even with gradient clipping. The right figure shows
the gradient before clipping and the gradient will be clipped so that
its norm is bounded by $`0.5`$.

## Appendix I QFR with Lazy Update

In this section, we will show a variant of QFR, which we coin it as the
lazy update version. In this version, the original dilated regularizer
is applied instead of the bidilated one. The advantage of this variant
is that the convergence only requires the proportion of $`\eta_{s}`$ and
$`\eta_{s}^{\rm anc}`$ to be a constant with Q-value and counterfactual
value, so that the convergence rate depends polynomially on the game
size.

The disadvantage is that importance sampling is needed for the
additional regularizer when using stochastic feedback (the dispersion of
feedback is not very large since the magnitude of the additional
regularizer is controlled by $`\tau`$). Moreover, when sampling a
trajectory, for those infosets that are not on the trajectory, we still
need to update the strategy $`\pi(\cdot{\,|\,}s)`$ in them. In other
words, for those infosets with $`\widetilde{q}^{(t)}=0`$, we still need
to update the strategy due to the additional regularizer (please refer
to
([I.4](#A9.Ex41 "In I.1 Lazy QFR with Stochastic Feedback ‣ Appendix I QFR with Lazy Update ‣ A Policy-Gradient Approach to Solving Imperfect-Information Games with Best-Iterate Convergence"))
for details). In practice, we can ignore the update of those infosets
that are not along the trajectory, and postpone the update till the next
time visiting them. That’s why we call it a lazy update.

Consider the following update-rule, which is
([4.2](#S4.E2 "Equation 4.2 ‣ 4.2 Convergence with Full Information Feedback ‣ 4 Analysis ‣ A Policy-Gradient Approach to Solving Imperfect-Information Games with Best-Iterate Convergence"))
with dilated regularizer instead of the bidilated one.

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\pi^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)\!=\!\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}}_{\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)\in\Delta_{|\mathcal{A}_{s}|}^{\gamma_{s},\bm{\nu}_{s}}}`$ | $`\displaystyle\left\langle\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s),-q^{(t-1)}(s,\cdot)\right\rangle+\frac{\tau}{m^{(t-1)}_{s}}\psi_{s}^{\Delta}(\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\eta_{s}}D_{\psi_{s}^{\Delta}}(\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s),\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))`$ |  | (I.1) |
|  | $`\displaystyle\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t+1)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)\!=\!\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}}_{\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)\in\Delta_{|\mathcal{A}_{s}|}^{\gamma_{s},\bm{\nu}_{s}}}`$ | $`\displaystyle\left\langle\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s),-q^{(t)}(s,\cdot)\right\rangle+\frac{\tau}{m^{(t)}_{s}}\psi_{s}^{\Delta}(\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\eta_{s}}D_{\psi_{s}^{\Delta}}(\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s),\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))`$ |  | (I.2) |

As
[Theorem 4.2](#S4.Thmtheorem2 "Theorem 4.2 (Informal). ‣ 4.2 Convergence with Full Information Feedback ‣ 4 Analysis ‣ A Policy-Gradient Approach to Solving Imperfect-Information Games with Best-Iterate Convergence"),
we can show the convergence of this update-rule. Note that in the
following theorem, the proportion of $`\eta_{s}`$ and
$`\eta_{s}^{\rm anc}`$ is now a constant when the feedback is Q-value or
counterfactual value, which implies a polynomial dependence on the game
size.

###### Theorem I.1.

Consider the update rule
([I.1](#A9.Ex1 "In Appendix I QFR with Lazy Update ‣ A Policy-Gradient Approach to Solving Imperfect-Information Games with Best-Iterate Convergence"))
and $`q^{(t)}(s,\cdot)`$ is chosen to be counterfactual value,
trajectory Q-value, or Q-value. When
$`\frac{\eta_{s}^{\rm anc}}{\eta_{s}}\leq\frac{\tau}{2C_{s}^{-}}`$ for
any $`s\in{\mathcal{S}}`$ and
[A](#A3.I1.i1 "Condition A ‣ Appendix C Stability of Trajectory Q-value and Q-Value ‣ A Policy-Gradient Approach to Solving Imperfect-Information Games with Best-Iterate Convergence"),[B](#A3.I1.i2 "Condition B ‣ Appendix C Stability of Trajectory Q-value and Q-Value ‣ A Policy-Gradient Approach to Solving Imperfect-Information Games with Best-Iterate Convergence"),[C](#A3.I1.i3 "Condition C ‣ Appendix C Stability of Trajectory Q-value and Q-Value ‣ A Policy-Gradient Approach to Solving Imperfect-Information Games with Best-Iterate Convergence")
are satisfied, we have the following guarantee.

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\sum_{t=2}^{T}D_{\psi^{\Pi}}(\bm{\mu}^{(\tau,\gamma),*},\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\bm{\mu}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)})`$ |  |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\leq`$ | $`\displaystyle 2\sum_{s\in{\mathcal{S}}}C_{s}^{/}\eta_{s}^{\rm anc}\mu^{(\tau,\gamma),*}(\sigma(s))\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left|\psi_{s}^{\Delta}(\pi^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))-\psi_{s}^{\Delta}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t+1)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))\right|`$ |  | (I.3) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+\frac{4}{\tau}\sum_{s\in{\mathcal{S}}}C_{s}^{\text{diff}}\mu^{(\tau,\gamma),*}(\sigma(s))\left\|\bm{q}\right\|_{\infty}\eta_{s}M_{2}T`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+\frac{2}{\tau}\sum_{s\in{\mathcal{S}}}\frac{M_{2}}{\eta_{s}}\mu^{(\tau,\gamma),*}(\sigma(s))D_{\psi_{s}^{\Delta}}(\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s),\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(1)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)).`$ |  |  |

###### Lemma I.2 (Generalized from Lemma C.2. in [Liu et al. \[2023\]](#bib.bib31)).

Consider the update rule in
([4.2](#S4.E2 "Equation 4.2 ‣ 4.2 Convergence with Full Information Feedback ‣ 4 Analysis ‣ A Policy-Gradient Approach to Solving Imperfect-Information Games with Best-Iterate Convergence")).
When $`\psi_{s}^{\Delta}`$ is strongly convex, then for any
$`\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)\in\Delta^{|\mathcal{A}_{s}|}`$ and
$`t\geq 1`$, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\eta_{s}\frac{\tau}{m^{(t)}_{s}}\psi_{s}^{\Delta}(\pi^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))-\eta_{s}\frac{\tau}{m^{(t)}_{s}}\psi_{s}^{\Delta}(\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+\eta_{s}\tau\left(\frac{1}{m^{(t-1)}_{s}}-\frac{1}{m^{(t)}_{s}}\right)(\psi_{s}^{\Delta}(\pi^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))-\psi_{s}^{\Delta}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t+1)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)))`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+\eta_{s}\langle-q^{(t)}(s,\cdot),\pi^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)-\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)\rangle`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\leq`$ | $`\displaystyle D_{\psi_{s}^{\Delta}}(\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s),\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))-(1+\eta_{s}\frac{\tau}{m^{(t)}_{s}})D_{\psi_{s}^{\Delta}}(\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s),\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t+1)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle-(1+\eta_{s}\frac{\tau}{m^{(t-1)}_{s}})D_{\psi_{s}^{\Delta}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t+1)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s),\pi^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle-D_{\psi_{s}^{\Delta}}(\pi^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s),\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))+\eta_{s}\left\langle q^{(t-1)}(s,\cdot)-q^{(t)}(s,\cdot),\pi^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t+1)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)\right\rangle.`$ |  |

The lemma’s proof is similar to
[Lemma E.2](#A5.Thmtheorem2 "Lemma E.2 (Generalized from Lemma C.2. in [ ] ). ‣ Proof Sketch. ‣ Appendix E Proof of Theorem ‣ A Policy-Gradient Approach to Solving Imperfect-Information Games with Best-Iterate Convergence").

Multiplying $`m^{(t)}_{s}`$ on both sides of Lemma
[E.2](#A5.Thmtheorem2 "Lemma E.2 (Generalized from Lemma C.2. in [ ] ). ‣ Proof Sketch. ‣ Appendix E Proof of Theorem ‣ A Policy-Gradient Approach to Solving Imperfect-Information Games with Best-Iterate Convergence"),
we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\eta_{s}\tau\psi_{s}^{\Delta}(\pi^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))-\eta_{s}\tau\psi_{s}^{\Delta}(\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))+\eta_{s}\tau\left(\frac{m^{(t)}_{s}}{m^{(t-1)}_{s}}-1\right)(\psi_{s}^{\Delta}(\pi^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))-\psi_{s}^{\Delta}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t+1)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)))`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+\eta_{s}m^{(t)}_{s}\langle-q^{(t)}(s,\cdot),\pi^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)-\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)\rangle`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\leq`$ | $`\displaystyle m^{(t)}_{s}D_{\psi_{s}^{\Delta}}(\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s),\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))-(m^{(t)}_{s}+\eta_{s}\tau)D_{\psi_{s}^{\Delta}}(\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s),\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t+1)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle-(m^{(t)}_{s}+\eta_{s}\tau\frac{m^{(t)}_{s}}{m^{(t-1)}_{s}})D_{\psi_{s}^{\Delta}}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t+1)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s),\pi^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle-m^{(t)}_{s}D_{\psi_{s}^{\Delta}}(\pi^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s),\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))+\eta_{s}m^{(t)}_{s}\left\langle q^{(t-1)}(s,\cdot)-q^{(t)}(s,\cdot),\pi^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t+1)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)\right\rangle.`$ |  |

By using Property
[2](#Thmproperty2 "Property 2 (Stability). ‣ 4.1 Preliminaries and Basic Properties ‣ 4 Analysis ‣ A Policy-Gradient Approach to Solving Imperfect-Information Games with Best-Iterate Convergence"),
we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\left(\frac{m^{(t)}_{s}}{m^{(t-1)}_{s}}-1\right)(\psi_{s}^{\Delta}(\pi^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))-\psi_{s}^{\Delta}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t+1)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)))`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\geq`$ | $`\displaystyle-C_{s}^{/}\eta_{s}^{\mathrm{anc}}\left|\psi_{s}^{\Delta}(\pi^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))-\psi_{s}^{\Delta}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t+1)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))\right|.`$ |  |

Furthermore, by using Lemma
[C.2](#A3.Thmtheorem2 "Lemma C.2. ‣ Proof. ‣ Lemma C.1 (Stability of 𝑚^(𝑡)_𝑠 under Euclidean regularizer). ‣ C.1 Stability of Trajectory Q-value ‣ Appendix C Stability of Trajectory Q-value and Q-Value ‣ A Policy-Gradient Approach to Solving Imperfect-Information Games with Best-Iterate Convergence")
and Hölder’s Inequality, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\left|\left\langle q^{(t-1)}(s,\cdot)-q^{(t)}(s,\cdot),\pi^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t+1)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)\right\rangle\right|`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\leq`$ | $`\displaystyle\left\|q^{(t)}(s,\cdot)-q^{(t-1)}(s,\cdot)\right\|_{\infty}\cdot\left\|\pi^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)-\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t+1)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)\right\|_{1}\leq 2C_{s}^{\mathrm{diff}}\left\|\bm{q}\right\|_{\infty}\eta_{s}.`$ |  |

where
$`\left\|\bm{q}\right\|_{\infty}=\max_{t\in[T],s\in{\mathcal{S}}}\left\|q^{(t)}(s,\cdot)\right\|_{\infty}`$.

By telescoping and non-negativity of Bregman divergence, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\eta_{s}\tau\psi_{s}^{\Delta}(\pi^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))-\eta_{s}\tau\psi_{s}^{\Delta}(\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))+\eta_{s}m^{(t)}_{s}\langle-q^{(t)}(s,\cdot),\pi^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)-\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)\rangle\right)`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\leq`$ | $`\displaystyle\sum_{t=2}^{T}\underbrace{\left(m^{(t)}_{s}-m^{(t-1)}_{s}-\eta_{s}\tau\right)}_{\hbox to7.56pt{\vbox to7.56pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 3.77788pt\lower-3.77788pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 4.95 0 C 4.95 2.73 2.73 4.95 0 4.95 C -2.73 4.95 -4.95 2.73 -4.95 0 C -4.95 -2.73 -2.73 -4.95 0 -4.95 C 2.73 -4.95 4.95 -2.73 4.95 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-1.99306pt}{-2.25555pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 -2.76 -3.12)} \pgfsys@hbox{61}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}D_{\psi_{s}^{\Delta}}(\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s),\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+C_{s}^{/}\eta_{s}\tau\eta_{s}^{\mathrm{anc}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left|\psi_{s}^{\Delta}(\pi^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))-\psi_{s}^{\Delta}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t+1)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))\right|+2C_{s}^{\mathrm{diff}}\left\|\bm{q}\right\|_{\infty}\eta_{s}^{2}M_{2}T`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+m^{(1)}_{s}D_{\psi_{s}^{\Delta}}(\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s),\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(1)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)).`$ |  |

![](data:image/svg+xml;base64,PHN2ZyBpZD0iQTkucDEwLm0xLnBpYzEiIGNsYXNzPSJsdHhfcGljdHVyZSBsdHhfbWFya2VkYXNtYXRoIiBoZWlnaHQ9IjEzLjM4IiBvdmVyZmxvdz0idmlzaWJsZSIgc3R5bGU9InZlcnRpY2FsLWFsaWduOi0yLjIzcHgiIHZlcnNpb249IjEuMSIgdmlld2JveD0iMCAwIDEzLjM4IDEzLjM4IiB3aWR0aD0iMTMuMzgiPjxnIHN0eWxlPSItLWx0eC1zdHJva2UtY29sb3I6IzAwMDAwMDstLWx0eC1maWxsLWNvbG9yOiMwMDAwMDA7IiBmaWxsPSIjMDAwMDAwIiBzdHJva2U9IiMwMDAwMDAiIHN0cm9rZS13aWR0aD0iMC40cHQiIHRyYW5zZm9ybT0idHJhbnNsYXRlKDAsMTMuMzgpIG1hdHJpeCgxIDAgMCAtMSAwIDApIHRyYW5zbGF0ZSg2LjY5LDApIHRyYW5zbGF0ZSgwLDYuNjkpIj48cGF0aCBzdHlsZT0iZmlsbDpub25lIiBkPSJNIDYuNDIgMCBDIDYuNDIgMy41NCAzLjU0IDYuNDIgMCA2LjQyIEMgLTMuNTQgNi40MiAtNi40MiAzLjU0IC02LjQyIDAgQyAtNi40MiAtMy41NCAtMy41NCAtNi40MiAwIC02LjQyIEMgMy41NCAtNi40MiA2LjQyIC0zLjU0IDYuNDIgMCBaIE0gMCAwIiAvPjxnIHN0eWxlPSItLWx0eC1zdHJva2UtY29sb3I6IzAwMDAwMDstLWx0eC1maWxsLWNvbG9yOiMwMDAwMDA7IiBmaWxsPSIjMDAwMDAwIiBzdHJva2U9IiMwMDAwMDAiIHRyYW5zZm9ybT0ibWF0cml4KDEuMCAwLjAgMC4wIDEuMCAtMy40NiAtNC40NikiPjxmb3JlaWdub2JqZWN0IHN0eWxlPSItLWx0eC1mby13aWR0aDowLjVlbTstLWx0eC1mby1oZWlnaHQ6MC42NGVtOy0tbHR4LWZvLWRlcHRoOjBlbTtmb250LXNpemU6MTBwdDsiIGhlaWdodD0iOC45MiIgb3ZlcmZsb3c9InZpc2libGUiIHRyYW5zZm9ybT0ibWF0cml4KDEgMCAwIC0xIDAgOC45MikiIHdpZHRoPSI2LjkyIj48c3BhbiBjbGFzcz0ibHR4X2ZvcmVpZ25vYmplY3RfY29udGFpbmVyIj48c3BhbiBjbGFzcz0ibHR4X2ZvcmVpZ25vYmplY3RfY29udGVudCI+PHNwYW4gaWQ9IkE5LnAxMC5tMS5waWMxLjEiIGNsYXNzPSJsdHhfdGV4dCI+MTwvc3Bhbj48L3NwYW4+PC9zcGFuPjwvZm9yZWlnbm9iamVjdD48L2c+PC9nPjwvc3ZnPg==)
can be upper-bounded by
$`C_{s}^{-}\eta_{s}^{\mathrm{anc}}-\eta_{s}\tau\leq-\frac{\eta_{s}\tau}{2}`$
by Property
[2](#Thmproperty2 "Property 2 (Stability). ‣ 4.1 Preliminaries and Basic Properties ‣ 4 Analysis ‣ A Policy-Gradient Approach to Solving Imperfect-Information Games with Best-Iterate Convergence")
and letting
$`\frac{\eta_{s}^{\mathrm{anc}}}{\eta_{s}}\leq\frac{\tau}{2C_{s}^{-}}`$.
By non-negativity of Bregman divergence, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\eta_{s}\tau\psi_{s}^{\Delta}(\pi^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))-\eta_{s}\tau\psi_{s}^{\Delta}(\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))+\eta_{s}m^{(t)}_{s}\langle-q^{(t)}(s,\cdot),\pi^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)-\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)\rangle\right)`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\leq`$ | $`\displaystyle-\frac{\eta_{s}\tau}{2}\sum_{t=2}^{T}D_{\psi_{s}^{\Delta}}(\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s),\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))+C_{s}^{/}\eta_{s}\tau\eta_{s}^{\mathrm{anc}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left|\psi_{s}^{\Delta}(\pi^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))-\psi_{s}^{\Delta}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t+1)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))\right|`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+2C_{s}^{\mathrm{diff}}\left\|\bm{q}\right\|_{\infty}\eta_{s}^{2}M_{2}T+m^{(1)}_{s}D_{\psi_{s}^{\Delta}}(\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s),\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(1)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)).`$ |  |

For simplicity, we use $`\bm{\mu}\coloneqq(\bm{x},\bm{y})`$,
$`F(\bm{\mu}^{\bm{\pi}})\coloneqq(-\bm{A}\bm{y},\bm{A}^{\top}\bm{x})`$,
$`\Pi\coloneqq\Pi_{1}\times\Pi_{2}`$, and
$`{\mathcal{S}}\coloneqq{\mathcal{S}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{S}}_{2}`$.

By using Lemma
[E.3](#A5.Thmtheorem3 "Lemma E.3 (Lemma 5.1 in [ ] ). ‣ Proof Sketch. ‣ Appendix E Proof of Theorem ‣ A Policy-Gradient Approach to Solving Imperfect-Information Games with Best-Iterate Convergence"),
we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle 0\overset{(i)}{\leq}`$ | $`\displaystyle G^{(T),\Pi}(\bm{\mu}^{(\tau,\gamma),*})`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle=`$ | $`\displaystyle\sum_{s\in{\mathcal{S}}}\mu^{(\tau,\gamma),*}(\sigma(s))G^{(T)}(h;\pi_{p(s)}^{(\tau,\gamma),*}(\cdot{\,|\,}h))`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle=`$ | $`\displaystyle\sum_{s\in{\mathcal{S}}}\mu^{(\tau,\gamma),*}(\sigma(s))\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Big(\tau\psi_{s}^{\Delta}(\pi^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))-\tau\psi_{s}^{\Delta}(\pi_{p(s)}^{(\tau,\gamma),*}(\cdot{\,|\,}h))`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+m^{(t)}_{s}\langle-q^{(t)}(s,\cdot),\pi^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)-\pi_{p(s)}^{(\tau,\gamma),*}(\cdot{\,|\,}h)\rangle\Big)`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\leq`$ | $`\displaystyle-\frac{\tau}{2}\sum_{t=2}^{T}\sum_{s\in{\mathcal{S}}}\mu^{(\tau,\gamma),*}(\sigma(s))D_{\psi_{s}^{\Delta}}(\pi_{p(s)}^{(\tau,\gamma),*}(\cdot{\,|\,}h),\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+\sum_{s\in{\mathcal{S}}}C_{s}^{/}\tau\eta_{s}^{\mathrm{anc}}\mu^{(\tau,\gamma),*}(\sigma(s))\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left|\psi_{s}^{\Delta}(\pi^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))-\psi_{s}^{\Delta}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t+1)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))\right|`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+2\sum_{s\in{\mathcal{S}}}C_{s}^{\mathrm{diff}}\mu^{(\tau,\gamma),*}(\sigma(s))\left\|\bm{q}\right\|_{\infty}\eta_{s}M_{2}T`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+\sum_{s\in{\mathcal{S}}}\frac{m^{(1)}_{s}}{\eta_{s}}\mu^{(\tau,\gamma),*}(\sigma(s))D_{\psi_{s}^{\Delta}}(\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s),\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(1)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)).`$ |  |

$`(i)`$ is because $`\bm{\mu}^{(\tau,\gamma),*}`$ is the NE of the
regularized and perturbed EFG. Then, by rearranging the terms, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\sum_{t=2}^{T}D_{\psi^{\Pi}}(\bm{\mu}^{(\tau,\gamma),*},\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\bm{\mu}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)})`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle=`$ | $`\displaystyle\sum_{t=2}^{T}\sum_{s\in{\mathcal{S}}}\mu^{(\tau,\gamma),*}(\sigma(s))D_{\psi_{s}^{\Delta}}(\pi_{p(s)}^{(\tau,\gamma),*}(\cdot{\,|\,}h),\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\leq`$ | $`\displaystyle 2\sum_{s\in{\mathcal{S}}}C_{s}^{/}\eta_{s}^{\mathrm{anc}}\mu^{(\tau,\gamma),*}(\sigma(s))\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left|\psi_{s}^{\Delta}(\pi^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))-\psi_{s}^{\Delta}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t+1)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))\right|`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+\frac{4}{\tau}\sum_{s\in{\mathcal{S}}}C_{s}^{\mathrm{diff}}\mu^{(\tau,\gamma),*}(\sigma(s))\left\|\bm{q}\right\|_{\infty}\eta_{s}M_{2}T`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+\frac{2}{\tau}\sum_{s\in{\mathcal{S}}}\frac{m^{(1)}_{s}}{\eta_{s}}\mu^{(\tau,\gamma),*}(\sigma(s))D_{\psi_{s}^{\Delta}}(\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s),\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(1)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)).`$ |  |

The first line is by Lemma
[4.1](#S4.Thmtheorem1 "Lemma 4.1 (Lemma D.2. in ( ) ). ‣ 4.2 Convergence with Full Information Feedback ‣ 4 Analysis ‣ A Policy-Gradient Approach to Solving Imperfect-Information Games with Best-Iterate Convergence").
Now, we achieved best-iterate convergence to the regularized NE
$`\bm{\mu}^{(\tau,\gamma),*}`$ in terms of Bregman divergence.∎

### I.1 Lazy QFR with Stochastic Feedback

Consider when we can only estimate $`q^{(t)}`$ at each iteration, we
apply the following update-rule to *each individual* infoset
$`s\in{\mathcal{S}}`$,

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\pi^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)\!=\!\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}}_{\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)\in\Delta_{|\mathcal{A}_{s}|}^{\gamma_{s},\bm{\nu}_{s}}}`$ | $`\displaystyle\left\langle\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s),-\widetilde{q}^{(t-1)}(s,\cdot)\right\rangle+\frac{\tau}{m^{(t-1)}_{s}}\psi_{s}^{\Delta}(\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\eta_{s}}D_{\psi_{s}^{\Delta}}(\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s),\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))`$ |  | (I.4) |
|  | $`\displaystyle\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t+1)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)\!=\!\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}}_{\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)\in\Delta_{|\mathcal{A}_{s}|}^{\gamma_{s},\bm{\nu}_{s}}}`$ | $`\displaystyle\left\langle\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s),-\widetilde{q}^{(t)}(s,\cdot)\right\rangle+\frac{\tau}{m^{(t)}_{s}}\psi_{s}^{\Delta}(\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\eta_{s}}D_{\psi_{s}^{\Delta}}(\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s),\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)).`$ |  |  |

###### Remark I.3.

Note that the update-rule above is not realistic, since even when an
infoset is unvisited at timestep $`t`$, *i.e.*
$`\widetilde{q}^{(t)}=0`$, we still need to update the strategy
$`\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t+1)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)`$
and $`\pi^{(t+1)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)`$ due to the regularization
term. However, we can do a lazy update. We will record the update time
$`t_{h}`$ of each infoset $`s\in{\mathcal{S}}`$. Once we touch a infoset
$`s\in{\mathcal{S}}`$ at timestep $`t`$, we will do $`t-t_{h}`$ steps
update with a zero $`q^{(t)}(s,\cdot)`$ and update the time stamp
$`t_{h}\leftarrow t`$.

Then, we have the following theorem. Note that the dependence on game
size is still polynomial for Q-value and counterfactual value, since
$`\frac{\eta_{s}^{\rm anc}}{\eta_{s}}`$ is bounded by a constant.

###### Theorem I.4.

Consider update-rule
([I.4](#A9.Ex41 "In I.1 Lazy QFR with Stochastic Feedback ‣ Appendix I QFR with Lazy Update ‣ A Policy-Gradient Approach to Solving Imperfect-Information Games with Best-Iterate Convergence"))
and $`q^{(t)}(s,\cdot)`$ is chosen to be counterfactual value,
trajectory Q-value, or Q-value. When
$`\frac{\eta_{s}^{\rm anc}}{\eta_{s}}\leq\frac{\tau}{2C_{s}^{-}}`$ for
any $`s\in{\mathcal{S}}`$ and
[A](#A3.I1.i1 "Condition A ‣ Appendix C Stability of Trajectory Q-value and Q-Value ‣ A Policy-Gradient Approach to Solving Imperfect-Information Games with Best-Iterate Convergence"),
[B](#A3.I1.i2 "Condition B ‣ Appendix C Stability of Trajectory Q-value and Q-Value ‣ A Policy-Gradient Approach to Solving Imperfect-Information Games with Best-Iterate Convergence"),
[C](#A3.I1.i3 "Condition C ‣ Appendix C Stability of Trajectory Q-value and Q-Value ‣ A Policy-Gradient Approach to Solving Imperfect-Information Games with Best-Iterate Convergence")
are satisfied, we have the following guarantee with probability
$`1-\delta`$.

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\sum_{t=2}^{T}D_{\psi^{\Pi}}(\bm{\mu}^{(\tau,\gamma),*},\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\bm{\mu}\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)})`$ |  |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\leq`$ | $`\displaystyle 2\sum_{s\in{\mathcal{S}}}C_{s}^{/}\eta_{s}^{\text{\rm anc}}\mu^{(\tau,\gamma),*}(\sigma(s))\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left|\psi_{s}^{\Delta}(\pi^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))-\psi_{s}^{\Delta}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t+1)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))\right|`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+\frac{4}{\tau}\sum_{s\in{\mathcal{S}}}C_{s}^{\text{diff}}\mu^{(\tau,\gamma),*}(\sigma(s))\left\|\bm{q}\right\|_{\infty}\eta_{s}M_{2}T`$ |  | (I.5) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+\frac{2}{\tau}\sum_{s\in{\mathcal{S}}}\frac{m^{(1)}_{s}}{\eta_{s}}\mu^{(\tau,\gamma),*}(\sigma(s))D_{\psi_{s}^{\Delta}}(\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s),\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu_{p(s)}^{(1)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))+\frac{8}{\tau}\left\|\bm{q}\right\|_{\infty}\cdot\left|{\mathcal{S}}\right|\sqrt{2T\log\frac{1}{\delta}}.`$ |  |  |

By Lemma
[4.3](#S4.Thmtheorem3 "Lemma 4.3 (Generalization of Proposition 1 in ( ) ). ‣ 4.3 Convergence with Stochastic Feedback ‣ 4 Analysis ‣ A Policy-Gradient Approach to Solving Imperfect-Information Games with Best-Iterate Convergence"),
once we have an unbiased estimator $`\widetilde{q}^{(t)}(s,\cdot)`$ for
$`q^{(t)}(s,\cdot)`$ in each infoset $`s\in{\mathcal{S}}`$ with the same
upper-bound $`\left\|\bm{q}\right\|_{\infty}`$, by
[Lemma 4.3](#S4.Thmtheorem3 "Lemma 4.3 (Generalization of Proposition 1 in ( ) ). ‣ 4.3 Convergence with Stochastic Feedback ‣ 4 Analysis ‣ A Policy-Gradient Approach to Solving Imperfect-Information Games with Best-Iterate Convergence"),
with probability $`1-\delta`$, we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\tau\psi_{s}^{\Delta}(\pi^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))-\tau\psi_{s}^{\Delta}(\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))+m^{(t)}_{s}\langle-q^{(t)}(s,\cdot),\pi^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)-\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)\rangle\right)`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\leq`$ | $`\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\tau\psi_{s}^{\Delta}(\pi^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))-\tau\psi_{s}^{\Delta}(\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))+m^{(t)}_{s}\langle-\widetilde{q}^{(t)}(s,\cdot),\pi^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)-\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s)\rangle\right)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+4\left\|\bm{q}\right\|_{\infty}\sqrt{2T\log\frac{1}{\delta}}`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\leq}`$ | $`\displaystyle-\frac{\tau}{2}\sum_{t=2}^{T}D_{\psi_{s}^{\Delta}}(\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s),\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))+C_{s}^{/}\tau\eta_{s}^{\rm anc}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left|\psi_{s}^{\Delta}(\pi^{(t)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))-\psi_{s}^{\Delta}(\mkern 1.5mu\overline{\mkern-1.5mu\pi\mkern-1.5mu}\mkern 1.5mu^{(t+1)}_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))\right|`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+2C_{s}^{\text{diff}}\left\|\bm{q}\right\|_{\infty}\eta_{s}m^{(t)}_{s}T`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+\frac{m^{(1)}_{s}}{\eta_{s}}D_{\psi_{s}^{\Delta}}(\pi_{p(s)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s),\pi_{p(s)}^{(1)}(\cdot{\,|\,}s))+4\left\|\bm{q}\right\|_{\infty}\sqrt{2T\log\frac{1}{\delta}}.`$ |  |

$`(i)`$ follows the discussion in Appendix
[E](#A5 "Appendix E Proof of Theorem ‣ A Policy-Gradient Approach to Solving Imperfect-Information Games with Best-Iterate Convergence").

Then, the proof follows the proof of Theorem
[4.2](#S4.Thmtheorem2 "Theorem 4.2 (Informal). ‣ 4.2 Convergence with Full Information Feedback ‣ 4 Analysis ‣ A Policy-Gradient Approach to Solving Imperfect-Information Games with Best-Iterate Convergence").
∎
````
